2026-10-25T19:22:50.6204011Z   QWEN_COMMIT_MESSAGE: [cluster:firmus] [nodes:2] Gate a wide exact Prefill QK-norm prologue and serve iteration 3 with the Prefill scheduler sync removal

Profile 40071892077 attributes 65.5 microseconds per layer to this prologue,
about 6.2 milliseconds per iteration. Its approximately 118 MB HBM payload
has a roughly 25 microsecond floor, motivating more memory-level parallelism
and less repeated trig traffic without adding floating-point work under the
Prefill power cap. Activation requires an all-GPU measured win.

Add the final runtime SGLang overlay for a non-image H128 prologue with four
token rows in flight per thread and 32 heads per CTA. Stage each token's
positions, cache locations and cosine/sine values once per head tile. Load
all four BF16 input tile pairs before arithmetic and combine adjacent lanes'
eight-byte FP8 segments into aligned 16-byte stores. At 4096 tokens this
reduces the launch from 9216 CTAs to 3072 and reduces repeated global trig
loads to three copies per token while retaining the same HBM payload.

Preserve the deployed FP32 product order, four partial sums, eight-lane XOR
reduction masks, final sum order, rsqrtf, BF16 rounding points and repaired
hardware FP8 conversion helper. Keep the original fast kernel and image
path intact. Read SGLANG_PREFILL_QKNORM in the host launcher; unset, control,
unknown values, image calls and head shapes outside 64 Q plus 4 KV retain
the existing launch.

Extend the existing four-rank Prefill selector after its FA3 measurements,
using the already-compiled serving JIT module and the same 175-second outer
budget. Give the added local screen a 14-second alarm and reject results
whose total screen time reaches 15 seconds. Test 3986, 4027 and 4096 tokens
with both int32 and int64 indices, packed serving strides, distinct Q/K
weights, large BF16 magnitudes, FP8 overflow boundaries, signed zeros and
subnormals. Use random positions through 8191 and unique random cache slots
including skipped slot zero. Compare all five complete output buffers as
uint8, including sentinel-filled untouched cache rows.

Collect 50 alternating CUDA-event timing pairs per shape and index type,
with eight prologues per graph replay to amortize host replay overhead.
Emit wide only when every shape and index type on all twelve physical
Prefill GPUs is byte-identical and saves at least 8 microseconds in median
per-call time. Otherwise emit control. Record the decision, rule, raw
samples and per-case savings in the existing selection artifacts and the
worker-sourced environment. Missing, malformed, timed-out or failed worker
fragments retain control.

Add CPU coverage for the exact threshold, per-rank/shape/index-type gates,
invalid timing samples, incomplete workers, malformed fragments, overlay
application and final overlay order. Update the existing ordering and
environment assertions for the new overlay and selection variable.

Compile the serving TVM-FFI entry points with CUDA 12.9.1 for sm_90a on the
Genkai login node, without a GPU or scheduler job. Both index types use 97
registers and 2112 bytes of shared memory for wide, versus 57 registers and
no shared memory for control; both have zero stack and zero spill bytes.
SASS confirms 128-bit global loads and stores. The remote work directory
was removed and its absence verified. No GPU performance or bitwise result
is claimed; the in-job gate supplies that evidence before activation.

Validation: the complete metrics CPU suite passes with 799 passed and 72
skipped, zero failures or errors. Ruff, Python and shell syntax, patch
application against the supplied serving tree, and git diff whitespace
checks pass. The final patch preserves the compiled CUDA-header hash.

This push serves iteration 3 on image 40197369083 without a build: the two
runtime overlays prefill-async-admission (e9540fd, expected -5 ms of GPU idle
per Prefill iteration) and prefill-qknorm-wide (gated in-job; control unless
byte-identical and >= 8 us faster on all 12 Prefill GPUs). Success is judged
by the Prefill iteration time in the worker logs (258 ms in 40201493911)
and the official score against 9036.12 / 9022.10.
